ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00089_d8e9818	Mr President. Mr. Federal Chancellor! Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! If we look at the development of the pandemic in this just over a year now, then, of course, there has been a great deal, and an example of this is clearly the masks. While the beginning of droplet infection, the oral nose protection was the appropriate, practically the spit protection that protects others. In the meantime, it is known that aerosols are at least as important, therefore the FFP2 masks that protect not only the opposite, but also one itself. This is a measure that is best compatible with most economic and social interactions, a reasonable measure, and I am grateful to everyone who adheres to it. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] As far as vaccination is concerned, it has been clearly demonstrated for all vaccines currently authorised that they prevent serious infections and, above all, deaths with very, very high probability. This is reflected in the studies, but it is currently also evident in real data in different countries. I am deeply grateful to Israel for providing anonymized data not only to the company, but also to the world public, and we can follow and learn from this ongoing monitoring that takes place there, which is very intense and conscientious. This is extremely important, and what you see there the Federal Chancellor has already indicated. What makes me particularly happy: The first effects are also seen in Austria. It is seen that the Covid-19 infections in the old and nursing homes are falling significantly. One can see that there is already a certain decoupling of the infection numbers on the one hand and the severe course on the other, which is due to the increasing protection of the risk groups, especially the elderly. I think this is a reasonable strategy. Especially now that we are also confronted with mutations, rapid vaccination is particularly important, as the longer this virus circulates worldwide, the greater the risk of new mutations occurring. Of course, the question is: Which vaccines work well against what variant? – That is constantly being checked. The same is true of mutations: How good is someone who has gone through the wild type of Covid-19 protected from a new mutant? – We can, of course, go faster in the development of the vaccine, and that will certainly be a challenge for the next few years. The fact that we are very far ahead of testing is a good way to disperse at this stage in which we are now epidemiological. It is clear that the figures do not rise exponentially in the form, as otherwise it would have been to be feared. What we all want in this country is a perspective, how we get out of this health crisis. We are still in a challenging time. This will continue for about the next ten weeks until the Vaccination can increasingly be offered to all those who want to be vaccinated. For this perspective, the government can set the course, and it does. But to make this perspective become reality, we are all called; that depends on how we all behave. I am grateful to everyone who behaves responsibly, who protects himself and others and helps us to realize this positive perspective until the summer. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] To show this perspective with our behaviour, we are all called – not only the government groups, but also the Social Democracy, NEOS, and I know that there are also many people among the liberals who together take this responsible path with us. Let's do it together, and we'll get it! Thank you.

It is a very solid thought, a thought of commonness. Not only the Corona pandemic confirms this worldwide effort, but also the refugee movement of 2015. They remember that about 1.3 million people, refugees, have come to Europe. Experts say that by 2050 another 200 million people will move. The reasons are known: Conflicts, wars, population explosion, lack of prospects in the Member States, but also climate change, droughts and floods. That is why the United Nations' Millennium Goals, which have successfully ended, have already been an important step. These were the predecessors of these Sustainable Development Goals. Just briefly: They have been successful in so far as there has been global efforts, for example in the fight against poverty. This has fallen worldwide from 1990 to 2015. Child mortality has fallen. The health of the birthed mothers increased during this period of comparison. People's access to clean drinking water has increased worldwide. That doesn't mean everything's reached, but the way is a real one, a more successful one. The setback by Corona is clear, but the assignment is that more needs to be done. ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00089_d8e17631	The word is now Mag.a Agnes Sirkka Prammer. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00089_d8e17655	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! We Here we deal with three requests, all of which go in the same direction: They indicate or they try to protect freedom of expression. This is an important concern for all of us. Therefore, the legitimate question is: Why do we not agree with these requests, why do we not support them? It's like this: We have just reviewed the Information Freedom Act, and this Information Freedom Act is an essential part of the law package against hatred on the web. We have made the decision that is always to be made in this area, namely, going to what happens when someone says something illegal with which he violates the rights of others. For this purpose, the State has two options: The one is, he can stay passive, can watch until the victim has taken his right to court and the illegal content is deleted. The alternative is, he demands an action, he demands to delete the illegal content. At first glance, it actually sounds like it doesn't make much difference, but it makes a huge difference for the victims. It is precisely the difference between self-defense and bystanders, how to spread hate postings. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] This difference does not infrequently mean to the victims of hatred in the net, either to continue living with animosities, with insults, with insults and with insults, or to be free again when the illegal content disappears, and to be able to move freely in the virtual as well as in the real world. These postings don't stay on the net, they're connected to the person. The person is known to people, has friends, has a family, has an environment, and they all read it. Therefore, it is important that these postings disappear from the web as soon as possible. We have opted for the path of maximum victim protection. That's why we're putting the platforms into duty. We demand the platforms to remove these postings as soon as possible. Do we restrict freedom of expression? – No, freedom of expression is just as designed; our basic state law has designed it that way. Freedom of expression does not include illegal, no insulting, no hateful messages. This is not part of freedom of expression. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] That has nothing to do with censorship. It doesn't decide which opinions you think are good or bad. It is only a matter of providing the victims with the greatest possible protection, of the perpetrators and of allowing platform operators to fulfil this obligation. We have opted for the path of victim protection, but we have set out mechanisms to ensure that there is no excessive regulation. There will be case law to this end. We will observe this case law closely and take a close look at it. With a look at Brussels, we are also curious about what will succeed with the digital service act. But what we do not want to constantly look at is timelines full of hate postings – and for that we have decided with the Information Freedom Act.

Questions 3 and 16: In the pre-purchasing contracts, non-binding delivery dates were mentioned by the manufacturers on a quarterly basis. However, these vary in part from the real delivery dates – I have mentioned this one example where we have significant deviations – because manufacturers always announce the actual delivery dates and quantities in the short term, because these factors depend on the actual production and delivery capacities of the manufacturers. You have to take into account: They have to go from zero to 150 in these weeks and supply the whole world. This is a major challenge in terms of production and logistics. Many are doing well, and one or the other has difficulties in doing so. Due to the inadmissibility of EMA authorisation decisions, access to raw materials, expected export restrictions et cetera, the delivery plans negotiated in the EU pre-purchasing contracts are due to Best Reasonable Efforts. Questions 6 to 11 and 20: This is a very important area because it represents the main lines. Mr Loacker asked: What strategy did you decide? Was Mr Auer, who always worked freely, independently, or did he have a baseline that was given? – Of course, there has been this and the answer is precisely about these issues, with your core question. In principle, the procurement process and the strategy behind it in Austria are essentially not different from the procurement process and the European Union's underlying strategy. In the second quarter of 2020, i.e. at the time of the relevant decisions, it was not clear which manufacturers with which technological platform can lead to a market authorisation when and in what quantities of vaccine. Furthermore, the European Union, as an assessment criterion with which manufacturers should be negotiated and concluded pre-purchasing contracts, used the earliest possible time availability – which was, of course, one of the most central, essential factors for our purchase decision – as well as the guaranteed quality in accordance with the high European standards of market authorisation and proportionality to the usual costs of vaccines. It has therefore always been assumed by me and my experts from a risk portfolio; in other words, the objective was to cover all technologies with a broad distribution and also to cover a situation that we see as not very likely, but which does not exclude, that one, two products do not receive a market authorisation. If we had not done this broad division and had, for example, just put it on two cards, we would have realized this very risk in a way that I believe is irresponsible. This was also the determination between me and the Federal Chancellor: to have sufficient vaccine for all persons living in Austria, i.e. to cover this total quantity on the basis of this risk portfolio, and third, with the criterion of as fast a delivery as possible, so that the relevant time – the first half – is best secured. For Austria, the quantities in the vaccine collection have always been calculated from the overall demand and not only from the number of epidemiological target groups: Austria has about 8.8 million inhabitants, of which about 7.5 million are people over 16 years of age, the so-called impracticable population. If we take the average rate of vaccination – we have adopted the EU standard, the EU target is 70 percent – the figure is 5.25 million people, and 70 percent of 8.8 million people would be 6,160 million people. Thus, the central wholesale order in the autumn of 2020 took place in a phase when there was no information about which vaccine will be authorised, when this authorisation will be granted, when it comes to deliveries and which deliveries actually take place. In the autumn of 2020, therefore, care was taken to obtain as wide a portfolio of vaccines as possible in sufficient quantities, in order to avoid any narrowness in the case of any individual vaccine failure at the time. The second strategic principle with the same objective was to cover all three technologies, since at the time it did not seem to be certain whether all new technologies proved to be successful. You can remember that there has been a massive skepticism, especially as regards Biontech/Pfizer and Moderna. Finally, a third principle was the assessment and assumption of which vaccines would receive the first authorisations and in which delivery would be possible in the first half of the year, because it was always our ambitious goal to be able to make a vaccination offer to any resident and to each resident by autumn 2021. Germany, for example, says that this vaccination offer should be available there until the 21st century. We are very ambitious on the road in our target orientation on September. [[Abg. Belakovich: They won't do this either!]] The Federal Government was regularly informed by Council of Ministers about precisely these points of the procurement process. ==References====External links== Reported in detail on September 2020. With the exception of the second contracts of Biontech/Pfizer and Moderna, the number of ordered doses has been set in late summer/ autumn 2020 with the aim of ensuring the full coverage of the total population of 8.8 million by combining several vaccines in accordance with the provisions of the Council of Ministers, but at least the rate of vaccination of around 70 percent of the population over 16 years old. Thus, the central wholesale order in the autumn of 2020 took place in a phase when there was no information about which vaccine will be authorised, when this authorisation will be granted, when it comes to deliveries and which deliveries will take place. In the autumn of 2020, it was precisely this reason that it was taken care to realize a wide range of portfolios as far as possible. Question 12: The legal basis lies in the Federal Financial Framework Act as well as in the Bundesfinanzgesetz. In the first half of 2020, the amount of expected expenditure was not to be assessed in any way during the first half of the year, so funding from the Covid-19 crisis management fund was planned. The initial cost framework of EUR 200 million was based on the assumption that contracts for vaccine supplies should be signed to the extent of around 24 million doses – this was our first planning basis. Since it was not yet clear whether more than one dose of immunisation would be required – not even we knew at the time – it should be ensured that sufficient vaccine is available to the vaccinated and impregnable people living in Austria. The framework was agreed and defined within the coalition and allocated in the Federal Finance Act 2021 of the UG 24 to an initial amount of 120 million euros and 80 million euros for the second year. As a result of the signing of a second contract with two suppliers and the withdrawal of two options from the initial contracts and the extension of the vaccine portfolio by two other manufacturers in January of this year, the cost framework has been increased to a total of EUR 388.3 million. As before, the cover was made from the Covid-19 crisis management fund. These costs include expenditure on the vaccine, but also on the corresponding accessories as well as the entire logistics and transport. The costs for vaccinations and infrastructure are not included in this framework, they are reimbursed via the purpose grant or ASVG. On the question 18: A budget ceiling of EUR 200 million was set with the Council of Ministers' presentation of 27/44 of 29.7.2020. This budget includes, in addition to the costs for the collection of vaccines, additional material costs as well as the share of Austria in the increase of ESI funds, which were additional support from the European Union for individual vaccine developments. The European Union, having exhausted the entire ESI framework, has asked Member States whether they are carrying out co-financing in the event of an increase, so that we can bring additional products into the boat – the last two that I have spoken about, for example, Valneva – and we have, of course, participated. I think it is a fundamental idea of European solidarity to carry out joint financing in this way. With the Council of Ministers' presentation 45/16 of 19.1.2021, the existing budget was increased by an additional 115.3 million euros in order to obtain additional quantities from the second contract with Biontech/Pfizer, thus preventing delays in delivery and delayed or even completely absent market registrations from other manufacturers, but at the same time allowing as many people to immunise as early as possible. The Council of Ministers' presentation 47/27 of 9.2.2021 increased the existing budget by an additional EUR 73 million. In total, it is now about 388.3 million. With this increase, it was ensured that Austria can also obtain its quantities from the second contract with Moderna. At the same time, budgetary precautions were also taken to obtain the pro-rata share from the pre-purchasing agreements with Novavax and Valneva, which were still under negotiation. On question 25: In the autumn of 2020, the basic principle of the procurement of vaccines was to minimise risk and to mix technology and to expect possible delivery times. Our aim was to obtain as much supplies as possible that were expected in the first half of 2021. At Johnson & Johnson, at the time of ordering, it was generally assumed that deliveries would not occur in the first half of the year. For example, at Johnson & Johnson 63 percent of the theoretically possible doses were ordered. The aim was to obtain so much vaccine to provide 8.8 million people with vaccination. In order to fulfil this task, the budget of 200 million euros was available at the time. Johnson & Johnson is based on the same technology as Astra Zeneca – this is a viral vector, and that was also a consideration: not always to realize a complete order from a particular technological line in the technology mix, but also to realize a risk distribution of the three different technologies – thus securing a second vaccine of this technology with Johnson & Johnson. With Astra Zeneca, almost three million people can be vaccinated and with Johnson & Johnson 2.5 million people. In total, Austria has secured approximately 8.4 million doses of the technology viral vector with the collected quantities of these two vaccines. This enables almost 5.5 million people to be given vaccination of this technology. With 70 percent of the population over 14 years of age, that would be 5.3 million people, it would be far more than 100 percent who could only be vaccinated with this viral vector technology. The starting point for how many vaccinations can be carried out with this technology was the following in summer, early autumn 2020: 2020: 200 000, Q1/2021: 1.8 million, Q2/2021: 4.1 million. A total of 3,6 million people were expected to be vaccinated by the end of the second quarter of 2021 alone with this technology in Austria. July 2020 and from the parliamentary materials to the Federal Finance Act and the so-called Empowerment Act, the Federal Act, with which authorizations are available via federal assets to cover the need for Covid 19 vaccinations and rapid tests. That is to say, we really do not have a quantity problem, since almost 31 million vaccines have been ordered, each with the aim of providing the main delivery in the first half of the year and at a risk compensation. As a result, not all vaccine manufacturers and contracts, especially those where a later delivery was expected than in the first half of the year, the full pro-rata share was obtained. Therefore, there have been such preliminary talks, but without appropriate safeguards and anchorages. Our level of conversation was and is always that we sit down in the middle of this year and then, when we're through, see how much vaccine is left, and then you can sell it on purpose. We don't have anything if we don't need him. We do not need him, but we paid him, others would need him, but we cannot buy him because they are not part of the European Union. Last, but not least to questions 55 to 62: Europe-wide vaccine procurement is a pan-European project, and I believe that it is absolutely right and positive. For example, from my point of view, we are now dealing with one of the producers who does not comply with his contracts much easier when we appear European. For a small state like Austria, such a dispute would be extremely difficult and probably less successful. This pan-European project has contributed significantly to the fact that so early in the pandemic, so many approved or approved vaccines are already available – they are mainly European products. I also find it very important that vaccination companies cooperate extremely intensively there, now also in the division of labour towards production, filling, extradition – even there are already the first mergers. This is, in principle, very, very positive from the basic point of view. We also owe this to the great achievements of research and science as well as to those of individual pharmaceutical companies. There are very clear responsibilities at European level, for example in the area of framework contracts, but also at national level in the field of national vaccinations. There, of course, my department is leading and in close coordination with those departments, which also make a significant contribution in this respect, unless it is in logistical implementation – in particular the Ministry of Defence – or in international voting, because the largest vaccination in the history of Austria is a national power act that we can only vote together. A common European approach also gives rise to additional possibilities, as was the last – you have mentioned – the advanced vaccine delivery of ten million doses of Biontech/Pfizer. The exact division of these doses is still subject to negotiations. According to the contracts, 200 000 cans would be available to us in this benefit – these are additional doses that are not yet included in this package, so it is an increase and acceleration that will be associated with them. Whether there is a further increase – the Chancellor said it could be 400 000 doses in total – will depend on the negotiations at the European Council. The precise distribution of these doses is still the subject of negotiations and will also influence whether there is a solution that primarily focuses on the population key, or whether at the same time a It is possible to compensate for differences between Member States in the current supply situation. Austria is clearly committed to a fair distribution of these doses of vaccine. Fighting the pandemic is a hercules task. That's not one thing, Mr. Loacker, that's just going on the team. ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00089_d8e26869	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen. The fact that a citizens' initiative or petition comes into the committee responsible is almost rare. The petition – which has already been reported – has already been brought together by representatives of FPÖ, NEOS, SPÖ and Greens about a year and a half ago, and numerous opinions have been received. Almost all the opinions have come to the conclusion that the preservation of free access to the sea for the public has an absolutely high priority for all those involved. In order to ensure free access to the sea in the future, however, it also needs very clear legal provisions. Many sea entrances belong to the Austrian Federal Forests and are therefore federal affairs according to the Federal Forest Act. Specific occasion for this petition was the new building of a property with two housing units by the Austrian Federal Forest on a public bathing ground in Weyregg am Attersee. Of course, when renting such apartments, a higher rent can be achieved if they have their own private access to the lake. This was also implemented by the Austrian Federal Forests, and thus the people, and thus the public, have been deprived of a piece of public access to the sea – by the Austrian Federal Forest. The need for a change in the Federal Forest Act is obvious. This shows not least the opinion received by Oberösterreich Tourismus GmbH, which shows that the Federal Forests can also pay public sea access, namely in the form of lease payments from the municipalities. In my opinion, the fact that municipalities have to buy public sea access with tax money is, in my opinion, contrary to the Federal Forest Act and is urgently amended. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Of course, it is all the more incomprehensible that this petition has not been given the opportunity to continue to deal with and deal with this issue in the Committee. It is obvious that, when it comes to the Austrian Federal Forest, the Government parties are not even willing to allow further discussion in the Committee. This discussion has been prevented from taking note of the Greens' request for assistance – I would like to say: for the time being it has been prevented. I'm sure it's not over yet. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00089_d8e30509	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, Values Advisors! One of the stated objectives of this Federal Government is to strengthen Austria's pioneering role in digitisation. This concerns, on the one hand, the provision of a modern digital infrastructure – keyword broadband development – but also the provision of modern administration. With the present Digitalisation Fund Act, we are creating the prerequisite for urgent steps to drive Austria forward in the digital field. In concrete terms, this law aims to provide up to 80 million euros for the years 2021 and 2022, which are intended to achieve the following three objectives: firstly, the implementation of the necessary and also contained in the government programme of IT consolidation in the federal government; secondly, the expansion of electronic services – e-government – which benefit citizens and entrepreneurs, and thirdly, the implementation of digitalisation projects to accelerate process processes. Most of these funds are to be spent on the IT consolidation already mentioned. Why is that so important? – We currently have the situation that we use a range of IT services in the federal government. For example, resorts often use different soft and hardware, data centers and service providers; a structure that has grown organic for many years, but provides space for many improvements and savings. An approximation of the IT used offers not only potential for cost savings, but also for improvements to a reliable security infrastructure, uniform quality of service and increased transparency. In this context, I would like to point out that we are also following a recommendation from the Court of Auditors on this measure, which only noted in its last year's report that IT consolidation should be implemented by the Federal Government as a project of high priority, for the reasons already mentioned. In addition to the necessary consolidation, however, the government is also concerned about improving and expanding the digital services provided by the eGovernment for citizens in order to digitise official channels and to satisfy a modern state. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In order to further strengthen Austria in the field of digitalisation, I ask for broad consent. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00089_d8e30642	Mr President. Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! My topic is the Federal Law, which amends the law of measurement and sequencing. I thought I'd have to look at what history looks like. ==References====External links==* Official website ==References====External links== In 1777 Maria Theresa ordered the cementing patent, so to speak, there were the first measurements in Austria: the Vienna Elle, the Vienna Pound or the Wiener Klafter. By the way, this would have been an ideal distance measure at 1,89 meters, but I think we are all happy that we have in the meantime adopted from these old units. It is also interesting that exactly 150 years ago the metric system was introduced in Austria, and I think that was a very crucial step, because it has united the units of measurement and, so to speak, internationalized us in the units of measurement. A similar process is on and now again. I have worked as an electric technician in a laboratory and learned what it means to measure, calibrate, eyes, and that is quite challenging, because there are some factors such as humidity, ambient temperatures and air pressure that have an impact on the measurements, and that is why it is important to define them well. In 2011, a process was launched in which SI base units should be defined at constants. These are constants from physics and nature. This process was completed in 2018, and we now have new definitions for these SI base units and implement an EU directive that these basic units also come into national law – in our law. This is very important, because the first is a further contribution to internationalisation, but the second is also a much more precise measure. We could, of course, do a lot more in the future. For example, we could do away with inches or crude oil units. I think, especially in the case of crude oil units or coal units, we agree with the energy transition that they are obsolete. We could expect meters or gigawatt hours in the future. That would, I believe, unify the whole story and make fewer mistakes. But this Eichgesetz also cleans up a little. Some things then don't have to be abandoned. This, of course, also reduces costs. That reduces the effort, and it's a contribution to a little less bureaucracy in this whole squirrel, and that can only come to us, I think. This facilitates the work in the laboratories, and it also ensures the quality of our measurement system for our companies and thus also the product quality in Austria. That is why I invite everyone to make their contribution and to decide on this new Eich Act so that we can implement a little red tape reduction in Austria, but also have a greater accuracy. Thank you.
